Then $W'=W_1VUW_2$ is equivalent to $W$, each letter of $W'$ exchanges the same two values from $\psi$ as the corresponding letter of $W$, $\mathrm{wt}_\psi(W')=\mathrm{wt}_\psi(W)$, and $W'$ is a cover path from $\psi$ if and only if $W$ is.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The letters of $U$ and $V$ commute, so the products agree. A letter of $U$ only moves positions untouched by $V$, so it finds the same values at its two positions whether or not $V$ has been applied before it, and symmetrically for $V$; the letters of $W_1$ and $W_2$ see the same permutations in both transposition words. The last assertion is Lemma \ref{lemma:degree}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Reversal] \label{lemma:reversal}
Work in $S_n$ and let $w_0$ be the longest element. Let $W=(t_1,\ldots,t_N)$ be a transposition word with product $\pi$, and let $W^{\mathrm{rev}}=(t_N,\ldots,t_1)$. Then the $i$-th letter of $W$ is a descent letter from $\psi$ if and only if the $(N+1-i)$-th letter of $W^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is a descent letter from $w_0\psi\pi$; hence $\mathrm{wt}_{w_0\psi\pi}(W^{\mathrm{rev}})=\mathrm{wt}_\psi(W)$, and $W$ is a cover path from $\psi$ if and only if $W^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is a cover path from $w_0\psi\pi$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The $(N+1-i)$-th step of $W^{\mathrm{rev}}$ goes from $w_0\psi_i$ to $w_0\psi_{i-1}=(w_0\psi_i)t_i$ and exchanges the values $w_0\psi_i(a_i)=n+1-\psi_{i-1}(b_i)$ and $w_0\psi_i(b_i)=n+1-\psi_{i-1}(a_i)$; the first is larger than the second if and only if $\psi_{i-1}(a_i)>\psi_{i-1}(b_i)$. Since $\ell(w_0v)=\ell(w_0)-\ell(v)$, the length change of this step is $\ell(\psi_i)-\ell(\psi_{i-1})$, the length change of the $i$-th step of $W$, so the two steps are covers of the same kind simultaneously (Definition \ref{def:cover}).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The token lemma}
Fix $C\geq k+2$ and write $T_x=t_{xK}$, $S_x=t_{xC}$ for $x\leq k$, and $Z=t_{KC}$; thus for every $x\leq k$ the three products $T_xS_x=S_xZ=ZT_x$ coincide (they are the $3$-cycle $(x,K,C)$ in some order), and likewise $T_xZ=ZS_x=S_xT_x$. Let $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$ be distinct positions $\leq k$, let $x_j$ be either a further position $\leq k$ (the \emph{tail case}) or $x_j=K$ (the \emph{$\mathcal M$ case}), and let $e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1}$ be distinct positions $\leq k$ different from $x_j$ (they may coincide with some of $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$). The \emph{junction word} attached to these data is the transposition word
$$J=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_j}\cdot S_{e_1}\cdots S_{e_{s-1}}\cdot S_{x_j},$$
where in the $\mathcal M$ case the letter $T_{x_j}=T_K$ is omitted and $S_{x_j}=Z$. For $0\leq m\leq j-1$ its \emph{$m$-th resolution} is
\begin{align*}
J_m&=S_{x_m}S_{x_{m+1}}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}\cdot T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{m-1}}\cdot T_{x_m}\cdot T_{e_1}\cdots T_{e_{s-1}}\cdot T_{x_j}\qquad(1\leq m\leq j-1),\\
J_0&=Z\,S_{x_1}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}\cdot T_{e_1}\cdots T_{e_{s-1}}\cdot T_{x_j},
\end{align*}
again with $T_{x_j}$ omitted in the $\mathcal M$ case. (In the language of the detours: the letter $S_{x_j}$ is converted into the token $Z$, which travels to the left through the letters $S_{e_{s-1}},\ldots,S_{e_1}$, turning them into $T_{e_{s-1}},\ldots,T_{e_1}$, and then through $T_{x_{j-1}},\ldots,T_{x_{m+1}}$, turning them into $S$'s, until it is absorbed by $T_{x_m}$, which it turns into $S_{x_m}T_{x_m}$; for $m=0$ it is not absorbed.) Lemma \ref{lemma:updown} is the case $s=1$ of the $\mathcal M$ case: there $J=F_{\mathrm{up}}$, $J_0=F_{\mathrm{std}}$ and $J_m$ is $F_m$ with its first two blocks interchanged.

\begin{lemma} \label{lemma:resolutionproduct}
Each $J_m$ is equivalent to $J$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The lengths agree. In the tail case, $T_{x_j}$ commutes with $S_{e_1},\ldots,S_{e_{s-1}}$, and $T_{x_j}S_{x_j}=ZT_{x_j}$; in the $\mathcal M$ case the last letter is $Z$ already. In both cases $J=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{j-1}}\,S_{e_1}\cdots S_{e_{s-1}}\,Z\,T_{x_j}$ (with $T_{x_j}$ omitted in the $\mathcal M$ case). Using $S_eZ=ZT_e$ repeatedly, $J=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{j-1}}\,Z\,T_{e_1}\cdots T_{e_{s-1}}T_{x_j}$. Using $T_xZ=ZS_x$ for $x=x_{j-1},\ldots,x_{m+1}$ and then $T_{x_m}Z=S_{x_m}T_{x_m}$, we get $T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{j-1}}Z=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{m-1}}\,S_{x_m}T_{x_m}\,S_{x_{m+1}}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}$ for $m\geq1$, and $=Z\,S_{x_1}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}$ for $m=0$. Finally the letters $S_{x_{m+1}},\ldots,S_{x_{j-1}}$ commute with $T_{x_1},\ldots,T_{x_m}$ and may be moved to the front; this gives $J_m$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Token lemma] \label{lemma:token}
Let $\psi\in S_\infty$ and suppose that the junction word $J$ is a cover path from $\psi$. Put $\beta_0=\psi(K)$, $\beta_l=\psi(x_l)$ for $1\leq l\leq j-1$, and $\gamma=\psi(C)$. Then the cyclic sequence $\Gamma=(\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$ is unimodal; let $g\in\{0,1,\ldots,j-1\}$ be the insertion gap of $\gamma$ in $\Gamma$ (gap $g\geq1$ is $(\beta_{g-1},\beta_g)$, gap $0$ is the wrap $(\beta_{j-1},\beta_0)$; $g=0$ if $j=1$).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] For $0\leq m\leq j-1$, the resolution $J_m$ is a cover path from $\psi$ with $\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J_m)=\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J)$ if and only if $m=g$.
\item[(ii)] If $e_t=x_l$ for some $t\leq s-1$ and $l\leq j-1$, then $l>g$. If moreover the transposition word $J\,S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}$ is a cover path from $\psi$ for some further distinct positions $e_{s+1},\ldots,e_r\leq k$ (different from $x_j$ and from $e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1}$), and $e_t=x_l$ for some $t\geq s+1$ and $l\leq j-1$, then $l\leq g$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $T=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{j'}}$, where $j'=j$ in the tail case and $j'=j-1$ in the $\mathcal M$ case, and $\beta_j=\psi(x_j)$ in the tail case. The transposition word $T$ is the pivot chain of the cycle $(x_{j'},\ldots,x_1,K)$, so by Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(i) its letter $T_{x_l}$ exchanges $\beta_l$ (at $x_l$) with $\beta_{l-1}$ (at $K$), and after $T$ the position $x_l$ carries $\beta_{l-1}$ and $K$ carries $\beta_{j'}$. The remaining letters $S_{e_1},\ldots,S_{e_{s-1}},S_{x_j}$ of $J$ form the pivot chain of the cycle $(x_j,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,C)$ (in the $\mathcal M$ case, of $(K,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,C)$) from $\psi'=\psi T$; put $\varepsilon_0=\gamma$, $\varepsilon_t=\psi'(e_t)$ for $t\leq s-1$, and $\varepsilon_s=\psi'(x_j)=\beta_{j-1}$ (in both cases). By Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(i) the letter $S_{e_t}$ exchanges $\varepsilon_t$ with $\varepsilon_{t-1}$ and $S_{x_j}$ exchanges $\varepsilon_s$ with $\varepsilon_{s-1}$. Since $J$ is a cover path, both cycles are tight (Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(iii)), so by Corollary \ref{corollary:cyclic} the cyclic sequences $(\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{j'})$ and $E=(\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_s)$ are unimodal; in particular $\Gamma$, a block of the first, is unimodal. We record
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wtJ}
\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J)=\prod_{l=1}^{j'}q_{x_lK}^{[\beta_l>\beta_{l-1}]}\ \prod_{t=1}^{s}q_{e_tC}^{[\varepsilon_t>\varepsilon_{t-1}]},
\end{equation}
where $[P]\in\{0,1\}$ is the truth value of $P$ and $e_s$ stands for $x_j$ (tail case) or $K$ ($\mathcal M$ case).

Now track $J_m$ for $m\geq1$. Its first block is the pivot chain of $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_m,C)$ from $\psi$: $S_{x_m}$ exchanges $\beta_m$ with $\gamma$, and $S_{x_l}$ ($m<l<j$) exchanges $\beta_l$ with $\beta_{l-1}$; afterwards $x_m$ carries $\gamma$, $x_l$ carries $\beta_{l-1}$ for $m<l<j$, and $C$ carries $\beta_{j-1}$. The block $T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{m-1}}$ exchanges $\beta_l$ with $\beta_{l-1}$ and leaves $\beta_{m-1}$ at $K$. Then $T_{x_m}$ exchanges $\gamma$ (at $x_m$) with $\beta_{m-1}$ (at $K$), after which $K$ carries $\gamma$ and $x_m$ carries $\beta_{m-1}$. Next, $T_{e_t}$ ($t\leq s-1$) exchanges the value at $e_t$ with the value at $K$. The value at $e_t$ is $\varepsilon_t$: if $e_t$ is none of the $x_l$ it is $\psi(e_t)=\psi'(e_t)$, and if $e_t=x_l$ ($l\leq j-1$) it is $\beta_{l-1}$ in the present transposition word (by the above, in each of the cases $l<m$, $l=m$, $l>m$) and also $\psi'(x_l)=\beta_{l-1}$. The value at $K$ is $\varepsilon_{t-1}$ by induction. Finally, in the tail case, $T_{x_j}$ exchanges $\beta_j$ (the position $x_j$ has not been touched) with $\varepsilon_{s-1}$. For $m=0$ the tracking is the same except that $Z$ exchanges $\beta_0$ with $\gamma$ and then $S_{x_l}$ ($1\leq l\leq j-1$) exchanges $\beta_l$ with $\beta_{l-1}$, after which $K$ carries $\gamma$. Hence, with $q_{aC}=q_{aK}q_{KC}$ for $a\leq k$ and \eqref{eq:wtJ},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wtJm}
\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J_m)=\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J)\cdot q_{x_mK}^{\,P_m}\;q_{x_jK}^{\,Q}\;q_{KC}^{\,a_m-a_E},
\end{equation}
where $P_0=0$, $P_m=[\beta_m>\gamma]+[\gamma>\beta_{m-1}]-[\beta_m>\beta_{m-1}]$ for $m\geq1$, $Q=[\beta_j>\varepsilon_{s-1}]-[\beta_j>\beta_{j-1}]-[\beta_{j-1}>\varepsilon_{s-1}]$ in the tail case and $Q=0$ in the $\mathcal M$ case, $a_m$ is the number of ascents of the sequence $(\gamma,\beta_m,\beta_{m+1},\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$ (for $m=0$: of $(\gamma,\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$), and $a_E$ is the number of ascents of $E=(\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_s)$.

\emph{$Q=0$.} In the tail case, the letter $S_{x_j}$ of $J$ is a cover of the permutation reached before it, in which $x_j$ carries $\beta_{j-1}$, $C$ carries $\varepsilon_{s-1}$, and the position $K$, which lies strictly between $x_j$ and $C$, carries $\beta_j$. By Definition \ref{def:cover}, if $\beta_{j-1}<\varepsilon_{s-1}$ then $\beta_j\notin(\beta_{j-1},\varepsilon_{s-1})$, and if $\beta_{j-1}>\varepsilon_{s-1}$ then $\beta_j\in(\varepsilon_{s-1},\beta_{j-1})$; in each case $[\beta_j>\varepsilon_{s-1}]=[\beta_j>\beta_{j-1}]+[\beta_{j-1}>\varepsilon_{s-1}]$.

\emph{$a_E=[\beta_{j-1}>\gamma]$.} The cyclic sequence $E$ is unimodal, so as a linear sequence it has at most one ascent; if it has none then $\varepsilon_s<\varepsilon_0$, and if its ascent is $(\varepsilon_{t-1},\varepsilon_t)$ then by Corollary \ref{corollary:cyclic} $\varepsilon_t>\cdots>\varepsilon_s>\varepsilon_0>\cdots>\varepsilon_{t-1}$, so $\varepsilon_s>\varepsilon_0$. Thus $a_E=[\varepsilon_s>\varepsilon_0]=[\beta_{j-1}>\gamma]$. The same argument applies to any linear block of a unimodal cyclic sequence: \emph{the block has an ascent if and only if its last entry exceeds its first.}

\emph{$P_m\geq0$, with equality if and only if $m=g$ $(1\leq m\leq j-1)$.} If $\beta_m<\beta_{m-1}$ then $P_m=0$ exactly when $\beta_m<\gamma<\beta_{m-1}$, and $P_m=1$ otherwise. If $\beta_m>\beta_{m-1}$, then $(\beta_{m-1},\beta_m)$ is the ascent of $\Gamma$, so $\beta_{m-1}=\min\Gamma$ and $\beta_m=\max\Gamma$, and $P_m=0$ exactly when $\gamma\notin(\beta_{m-1},\beta_m)$, i.e.\ when $\gamma$ lies outside $[\min\Gamma,\max\Gamma]$; again $P_m=1$ otherwise. By the definition of the insertion gap, in both cases $P_m=0$ if and only if gap $m$ is the insertion gap of $\gamma$ in $\Gamma$.

(i) Since the monomials $q_{x_mK}$, $q_{x_jK}$ involve only $q_i$ with $i<K$ and $q_{KC}$ only $q_i$ with $i\geq K$, and $Q=0$, \eqref{eq:wtJm} shows that $\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J_m)=\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J)$ if and only if $P_m=0$ and $a_m=a_E$; by Lemmas \ref{lemma:resolutionproduct} and \ref{lemma:degree} this is also equivalent to $J_m$ being a cover path of weight $\mathrm{wt}_\psi(J)$. By the previous step, $P_m=0$ holds for $m=0$ and for $m=g$ (if $g\geq1$) only. Let $\Sigma$ be the unimodal cyclic sequence obtained by inserting $\gamma$ into its insertion gap of $\Gamma$.

If $g\geq1$: the sequence $(\gamma,\beta_g,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$ is a block of $\Sigma$, so $a_g=[\beta_{j-1}>\gamma]=a_E$, and $J_g$ qualifies. For $m=0$ we show $a_0\neq a_E$. Let $d\in\{0,1\}$ be the number of linear ascents of $(\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$, so that $a_0=[\beta_0>\gamma]+d$. If $d=0$ then $\beta_0>\cdots>\beta_{j-1}$ and the wrap is the ascent of $\Gamma$; as $g\neq0$, $\gamma$ lies in $(\beta_{j-1},\beta_0)$, so $a_0=1$ while $a_E=[\beta_{j-1}>\gamma]=0$. If $d=1$, the wrap $(\beta_{j-1},\beta_0)$ is a descent gap, and $g\neq0$ means $\gamma\notin(\beta_0,\beta_{j-1})$: if $\gamma<\beta_0$ then $a_0=2\neq a_E$, and if $\gamma>\beta_{j-1}$ then $a_0=1$ while $a_E=0$. So $J_0$ does not qualify, and $J_g$ is the unique resolution that does.

If $g=0$: only $m=0$ is possible, and $(\gamma,\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$ is the whole of $\Sigma$ read from $\gamma$, a linear block of a unimodal cyclic sequence, so $a_0=[\beta_{j-1}>\gamma]=a_E$ and $J_0$ qualifies.

(ii) Recall that a cyclic subsequence of a unimodal cyclic sequence is unimodal, and that a unimodal cyclic sequence of three distinct numbers is cyclically decreasing, i.e.\ reads $(\max,\mathrm{mid},\min)$ up to rotation; so two unimodal cyclic sequences containing the same three distinct numbers list them in the same cyclic order. Let $e_t=x_l$ with $t\leq s-1$, $l\leq j-1$, so that $\varepsilon_t=\beta_{l-1}$, and suppose $l\leq g$ (so $g\geq1$). In $\Sigma=(\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{g-1},\gamma,\beta_g,\ldots,\beta_{j-1})$ the three distinct numbers $\beta_{l-1},\gamma,\beta_{j-1}$ occur in the cyclic order $(\beta_{l-1},\gamma,\beta_{j-1})$, because $l-1\leq g-1$ and $l-1<j-1$. In the unimodal cyclic sequence $E$ they occur as $\varepsilon_0=\gamma$, $\varepsilon_t=\beta_{l-1}$, $\varepsilon_s=\beta_{j-1}$ with $0<t<s$, i.e.\ in the cyclic order $(\gamma,\beta_{l-1},\beta_{j-1})$, which is the opposite one; this contradiction proves $l>g$. For the second assertion, the whole cycle $(e_r,\ldots,e_1,C)$ is tight from $\psi'$ and its cyclic value sequence $(\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_r)$, with $\varepsilon_t=\psi'(e_t)=\beta_{l-1}$ for $e_t=x_l$, is unimodal. Suppose $e_t=x_l$ with $t\geq s+1$ and $g<l\leq j-1$. In $\Sigma$ the numbers $\gamma,\beta_{l-1},\beta_{j-1}$ occur in the cyclic order $(\gamma,\beta_{l-1},\beta_{j-1})$, because $g\leq l-1<j-1$; in the $e$-sequence they occur as $\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_s,\varepsilon_t$ with $0<s<t$, i.e.\ in the order $(\gamma,\beta_{j-1},\beta_{l-1})$, the opposite one. Hence $l\leq g$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{States and the junction process}
Fix $u,w\in S_\infty$ and a monomial $q$. By Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(iii), a level-$k$ step $v\tom{k}v'$ may be represented by any \emph{pivot word} of $v^{-1}v'$, i.e.\ any transposition word obtained by interleaving the pivot factorizations of its cycles; all of them are cover paths from $v$ of weight $q_k(v,v')$, and the same holds for level-$(k+1)$ steps. A \emph{state} is a transposition word $U=W_1\,|\,W_2$ with a cut, which is a cover path from $u$ of weight $q$ with product $u^{-1}w$, together with an \emph{active position} $x$, such that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(S1)] $W_1=R_1\,T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_j}$, where $R_1$ is a pivot word of a product of pairwise disjoint cycles, each consisting of its top, which is $\geq k+2$, together with positions $\leq k$, and $x_1,\ldots,x_j$ are distinct positions $\leq k$ with $x_j=x$ and $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}\notin\supp R_1$ (the active position may or may not lie in $\supp R_1$); or, in the \emph{$\mathcal M$ case}, $x=K$ and $W_1=R_1T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{j-1}}$ with $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$ as before.
\item[(S2)] $W_2=E\,W_2'$, where $E=S_{e_1}\cdots S_{e_r}$ is the pivot factorization of a cycle $e=(e_r,\ldots,e_1,C)$ with top $C\geq k+2$ containing $x$, say $x=e_s$, whose other elements $e_t$ ($t\neq s$) are positions $\leq k$; and $W_2'$ is a pivot word of a product of pairwise disjoint cycles, each consisting of its top, which is $\geq K$, together with positions $\leq k$, disjoint from $e$ and not containing $x$.
\item[(S3)] The cycle of $W_2'$ with top $K$, if there is one, is disjoint from $\{x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}\}$.
\end{enumerate}
Here $T_y=t_{yK}$ and $S_y=t_{yC}$ as before (with $C$ the top of $e$). Conditions (S1) and (S2) say that every cycle of $R_1$ and of $W_2'$, and in the tail case also $e$, has exactly one element greater than $k$, as the cycles of a level-$k$ step do, while in the $\mathcal M$ case $e$ has exactly one element greater than $K$; the requirement on the tops alone would not suffice, since a cycle such as $(K,C')$ with $C'\geq k+2$ has top $\geq K$ but is not a cycle of any level-$k$ or level-$(k+1)$ step. Two states are identified if they differ by reorderings as in Lemma \ref{lemma:inert}, i.e.\ we regard a state as the data (cycles of $R_1$, the sequence $x_1,\ldots,x_j$, the cycle $e$, the cycles of $W_2'$) rather than as a particular interleaving. A state is a \emph{source} if $x\notin\supp R_1$ (and the active position is in $[1,k]$ or $K$ accordingly); the sources are exactly the blocked tails and the chains of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$: a blocked tail $u\tom{k}v\tom{k}w$ with block data $(\rho_1,X,\rho_2)$ and cut $i=i_{\min}>0$ is the state with $R_1$ a pivot word of $\rho_1$, $(x_1,\ldots,x_j)=(x_1,\ldots,x_i)$, $e$ the cycle of $\rho_2$ through $x_i$, and $W_2'$ a pivot word of $d_i\rho_2e^{-1}$; a chain $u\tom{k}v\tom{k+1}w$ of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$ with $X=(x_n,\ldots,x_1,K)$ is the state with $(x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1})=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$, $x=K$, $e$ the cycle of $v^{-1}w$ through $K$, and $W_2'$ a pivot word of the other cycles. In both cases (S1) and (S2) hold because the two steps are $k$-shaped, respectively $k$- and $(k+1)$-shaped (the cycles of $\rho_1$, $\rho_2$ and $v^{-1}w$ other than those through $K$ have tops $\geq k+2$); (S3) holds for a blocked tail because its top-$K$ cycle $d_i=(x_n,\ldots,x_{i+1},K)$ of $W_2'$ avoids $x_1,\ldots,x_{i-1}$, and vacuously for a chain of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$, whose $W_2'$ contains no cycle through $K$. Conversely such a state is a blocked tail (resp.\ a chain of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$) of weight $q$: since $x\notin\supp R_1$, (S1) and (S2) make $W_1$ and $W_2$ pivot words of $k$-shaped permutations (in the $\mathcal M$ case, of a $k$-shaped and a $(k+1)$-shaped permutation), which are tight because $U$ is a cover path (Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(iii)), so $u\tom{k}v\tom{k}w$ (resp.\ $u\tom{k}v\tom{k+1}w$ with $K$ moved) for $v=uW_1$; in the tail case the pair is compatible, its two cycles with top $K$, namely $(x_j,\ldots,x_1,K)$ and the top-$K$ cycle of $W_2'$, meeting only in $K$ by (S3) and $x\notin\supp W_2'$, and cycles with a common top $\geq k+2$ sharing no element $\leq k$ by Lemma \ref{lemma:overlap}; and its cut $j$ is $i_{\min}$, which is positive, because $x_j=x\in\supp e\subseteq\supp\rho_2$. For a state $U$ with active position $x$ put
$$N_1(U)=\supp R_1\cup\{x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}\},\qquad M_2(U)=\{a\leq k: a\text{ is moved by }W_2\},$$
the set of positions $\leq k$ moved by $W_1$ other than (possibly) the active one, and the set of positions $\leq k$ moved by $W_2$.

The \emph{junction move} transforms a state $U$ as follows. The transposition word $J=T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_j}\,S_{e_1}\cdots S_{e_{s-1}}\,S_x$ formed by the $K$-letters of $W_1$ and the first $s$ letters of $E$ is a junction word in the sense of the token lemma (tail case if $x\leq k$, $\mathcal M$ case if $x=K$), it occupies a contiguous segment of $U$, and it is a cover path from $\psi=uR_1$. Let $g$ be the index provided by Lemma \ref{lemma:token}, and replace $J$ by $J_g$; by Lemmas \ref{lemma:degree}, \ref{lemma:resolutionproduct} and \ref{lemma:token}, the resulting transposition word
$$U^+=\begin{cases}R_1\,S_{x_g}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}\,T_{x_1}\cdots T_{x_{g-1}}\ \big|\ T_{x_g}\,T_{e_1}\cdots T_{e_{s-1}}\,T_x\,S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}\,W_2'&(g\geq1),\\[3pt]
R_1\,Z\,S_{x_1}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}\ \big|\ T_{e_1}\cdots T_{e_{s-1}}\,T_x\,S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}\,W_2'&(g=0),\end{cases}$$
with $T_x$ omitted when $x=K$ and with the cut placed as indicated, is a cover path from $u$ of weight $q$ with product $u^{-1}w$.

\begin{proposition}[The junction process] \label{prop:process}
Let $U$ be a state with active position $x$ and let $U^+$ be obtained by the junction move, with index $g$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] If $g=0$, then $U^+$ is a chain $u\tom{k+1}v^+\tom{k}w$ of $\mathcal M'^{\,\mathrm{mov}}_k$ of weight $q$, with triple $\big(q,\ u(\overline{N_1(U)}),\ w(\overline{M_2(U)})\big)$, where $\overline S=[1,k]\setminus S$.
\item[(b)] If $g\geq1$ and $x_g$ is not moved by $S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}W_2'$, then $U^+$ is a compatible pair $u\tom{k}v^+\tom{k}w$ of weight $q$ which is the blocked head of its block, with $T(v^+)=\big(q,\ u(\overline{N_1(U)}),\ w(\overline{M_2(U)})\big)$.
\item[(c)] If $g\geq1$ and $x_g$ is moved by $S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}W_2'$, let $f$ be the cycle of $S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}W_2'$ through $x_g$ (so $f$ is either $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$ or a cycle of $W_2'$). Then the transposition word $U'$ obtained from $U^+$ by moving the cut one letter to the right, past $T_{x_g}$, and moving the letters of $f$ to the front of the suffix, is a state with active position $x_g$, with $N_1(U')=N_1(U)$ and $M_2(U')=M_2(U)$, and the index $j$ of its junction word is $g<j$.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, starting from a source and iterating the junction move, one reaches after at most $j$ moves a target (a blocked head or a chain of $\mathcal M'^{\,\mathrm{mov}}_k$) of weight $q$ whose triple is the triple $S(v)$ of the source if the source is a blocked tail $v$, and the triple of the source if the source is a chain of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$. We write $\Phi(U)$ for this target.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
We first collect what Lemma \ref{lemma:token}(ii) says about the positions $e_t$ which are among $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$: if $t<s$ then $e_t=x_l$ with $l>g$, and if $t>s$ then $e_t=x_l$ with $l\leq g$. (The second assertion of (ii) applies because $JS_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}$ is a segment of the cover path $U$.) In particular $x_g\notin\{e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1}\}$, and $\{e_{s+1},\ldots,e_r\}\cap\{x_{g+1},\ldots,x_{j-1}\}=\emptyset$.

\emph{The prefix of $U^+$.} For $g\geq1$ it is $R_1$ followed by the pivot factorization of the cycle $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_g,C)$ and the pivot factorization of $(x_{g-1},\ldots,x_1,K)$. The positions $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$ avoid $\supp R_1$ by (S1), so if $R_1$ has no cycle with top $C$ these are three pairwise disjoint pieces, and if $R_1$ contains a cycle $(z_t,\ldots,z_1,C)$, then its letters followed by $S_{x_g}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}$ form the pivot factorization of the cycle $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_g,z_t,\ldots,z_1,C)$, disjoint from the other cycles. Thus the prefix is a pivot word of a product $\pi_1^+$ of pairwise disjoint cycles, each with exactly one element $>k$ (by (S1), the cycles of $R_1$ have this property, and the two new cycles have elements $x_l\leq k$ besides their tops), i.e.\ $\pi_1^+$ is $k$-shaped; being an initial segment of a cover path it is $u$-tight (Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(iii)), so $u\tom{k}v^+:=u\pi_1^+$. The positions $\leq k$ it moves form $N_1(U)$. For $g=0$ the prefix is $R_1$ followed by $Z S_{x_1}\cdots S_{x_{j-1}}$, which merges with the cycle of $R_1$ with top $C$ (if any) into the cycle $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_1,K,z_t,\ldots,z_1,C)$; so the prefix is a pivot word of a $(k+1)$-shaped, $u$-tight permutation moving $K$, i.e.\ $u\tom{k+1}v^+$ with $K$ moved, and it moves the positions $N_1(U)\cup\{K\}$.

\emph{The suffix of $U^+$.} Its letters $T$ are $T_{x_g}$ (if $g\geq1$), $T_{e_1},\ldots,T_{e_{s-1}}$, $T_x$ (if $x\leq k$), and the letters $T$ of $W_2'$, which form the pivot factorization of a cycle $D$ with top $K$ (trivial if $x=K$, since then $K\in e$ and $W_2'$ is disjoint from $e$ by (S2)). The positions $x_g,e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1},x$ and those of $D$ are pairwise distinct ($x_g\notin\{e_t\}_{t<s}$ by the first paragraph; $x\notin\{e_t\}_{t\neq s}$; $D$ is disjoint from $e$ and does not contain $x$ by (S2), and does not contain $x_g$ by (S3)), so these letters form, in the order in which they occur, the pivot factorization of the cycle $D^+=(D,\ x,\ e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,\ x_g,\ K)$ (we list the elements following the cycle from the top), respectively $(D,x,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,K)$ when $g=0$ and $(D,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,x_g,K)$ in the $\mathcal M$ case; by (S2) all its elements other than $K$ are $\leq k$. The remaining letters of the suffix are those of $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$ and of the other cycles of $W_2'$, which are pairwise disjoint, disjoint from $D$, $x$ and $e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1}$, have, by (S2), exactly one element greater than $k$, and, under the hypothesis of (a) or (b), do not contain $x_g$. Hence in cases (a) and (b) the suffix is a pivot word of a $k$-shaped permutation $\pi_2^+$, which is $v^+$-tight because the suffix is a terminal segment of a cover path; so $v^+\tom{k}w$. The positions $\leq k$ moved by the suffix are $M_2(U)$ in case (a) and $M_2(U)\sqcup\{x_g\}$ in case (b).

(a) By the above, $U^+$ is a chain $u\tom{k+1}v^+\tom{k}w$ whose first step moves $K$; its weight is $q$ and its triple is $(q,\fx_{k+1}(u,v^+),\fx_k(v^+,w))=(q,u(\overline{N_1(U)}),w(\overline{M_2(U)}))$.

(b) We have $u\tom{k}v^+\tom{k}w$. The two steps are compatible: the cycles with top $K$ are $(x_{g-1},\ldots,x_1,K)$ and $D^+$, which meet only in $K$ (as $e_t=x_l$ with $t<s$ forces $l>g$); and by Lemma \ref{lemma:overlap} two cycles of the two steps with a common top $\geq k+2$ cannot share an element $\leq k$. The block data of the pair are $\big(\rho_1^+,\ X^+=(x_{g-1},\ldots,x_1,K)D^+,\ \rho_2^+\big)$ with cut $g-1$, where $\rho_1^+$ contains the cycle through $x_g$ with top $C$; since $x_g$ is the $g$-th pivot of $X^+$, $i_{\max}=g-1$, so the pair is the head of its block and this head is blocked. Finally $\fx_k(u,v^+)=u(\overline{N_1(U)})$, $\fx_k(v^+,w)=w(\overline{M_2(U)\sqcup\{x_g\}})$ and $c(v^+)=v^+(K)=\beta_{g-1}$, the value at $K$ after the prefix; the letter $T_{x_g}$ moves this value to $x_g$, which is not moved by any later letter, so $w(x_g)=c(v^+)$ and $T(v^+)=(q,u(\overline{N_1(U)}),w(\overline{M_2(U)}))$.

(c) Since $x_g\notin D$ by (S3), $f\neq D$: so $f$ is either $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$ or a cycle of $W_2'$ other than $D$, and in either case, by (S2), $f$ consists of its top, which is $\geq k+2$, together with positions $\leq k$, one of them $x_g$. The prefix of $U'$ is the prefix of $U^+$ followed by $T_{x_g}$: it has the form (S1) with $R_1'$ the prefix of $U^+$ minus its letters $T$ (a pivot word of the cycles of $R_1$ other than the one with top $C$, if any, together with $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_g,z_t,\ldots,z_1,C)$; each of these consists of a top $\geq k+2$ and positions $\leq k$, by (S1) for $R_1$), with $K$-letters $T_{x_1},\ldots,T_{x_{g-1}},T_{x_g}$, and active position $x_g\in\supp R_1'$; the positions $x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1}$ avoid $\supp R_1'$. The letters of $f$ move no position moved by the letters of the suffix of $U^+$ which they pass (these letters are $T_{x_g}$---not passed---$T_{e_1},\ldots,T_{e_{s-1}},T_x$, the letters of $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$ if $f\neq$ this cycle, and letters of cycles of $W_2'$ other than $f$; all are disjoint from $f$ because the cycles of the suffix are pairwise disjoint apart from the common element $x_g$ of $T_{x_g}$ and $f$), so by Lemma \ref{lemma:inert} $U'$ is a cover path of weight $q$. Its suffix is $f$ followed by a pivot word of pairwise disjoint cycles: the cycle $D'=(D,x,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,K)$ formed by the letters $T_{e_1},\ldots,T_{e_{s-1}},T_x$ and those of $D$ (the letter $T_{x_g}$ having moved to the prefix), the cycle $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$ if $f$ is not this cycle, and the cycles of $W_2'$ other than $f$; all of these are disjoint from $f$, none of them contains $x_g$, and each consists of a top $\geq K$ together with positions $\leq k$. This is (S2) for $U'$, with $e'=f$ and $x'=x_g$. For (S3), the top-$K$ cycle of the new $W_2'$ is $D'$, and it avoids $x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1}$: $D$ does by (S3) for $U$, $x=x_j$ does, and $e_t=x_l$ with $t<s$ forces $l>g$ by the first paragraph. So $U'$ is a state with active position $x_g\leq k$, whose junction word has $g$ letters $T$ and index $g<j$. Its sets are $N_1(U')=\supp R_1'\cup\{x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1}\}=\supp R_1\cup\{x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}\}=N_1(U)$ and $M_2(U')=M_2(U)\cup\{x_g\}=M_2(U)$, since $x_g\in\supp f\subseteq M_2(U)$.

For the consequence, note that a source $U$ has $N_1(U)=M_1\setminus\{x\}$ where $M_1$ is the set of positions $\leq k$ moved by its first step, and $M_2(U)=M_2$ is the set moved by its second step. If $U$ is a blocked tail $v$, then $S(v)=(q,\fx_k(u,v)\cup\{c(v)\},\fx_k(v,w))$ with $c(v)=u(x)$ (Lemma \ref{lemma:blocks}(iii)), which is $(q,u(\overline{M_1\setminus\{x\}}),w(\overline{M_2}))$; if $U$ is a chain of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$ its triple is $(q,\fx_k(u,v),\fx_{k+1}(v,w))=(q,u(\overline{M_1}),w(\overline{M_2}))$, and $N_1(U)=M_1$ as $x=K$. Since the junction move strictly decreases the index $j$ of the junction word, the iteration terminates, by (a) or (b), after at most $j$ moves, and the invariance of $N_1$ and $M_2$ in (c) gives the triple.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[A junction move] \label{example:junction}
Let $k=1$, $K=2$, $u=123$ and $w=312$, so that $u^{-1}w=312=t_{12}t_{13}$. The steps $u\tom1v$ are the trivial one and $t_{12}$ ($v=213$; $t_{13}$ is not a cover of $123$ because of the intermediate value $2$), and the only compatible pair $u\tom1v\tom1w$ is
$$123\xrightarrow{t_{12}}213\xrightarrow{t_{13}}312,$$
with weight $q=1$ and $\omega_1=[\emptyset\mid\emptyset]=1$. Its block data are $\rho_1=1$, $X=(x_1,K)=(1,2)$, $\rho_2=(1,3)$, with cut $i=1=n$; since $x_1=1\in\supp\rho_2$ we have $i_{\min}=1$, so $v=213$ is a \emph{blocked tail}, and since $\supp\rho_1=\emptyset$, $i_{\max}=1$ and $v$ is also an unblocked head. Its triples are $S(v)=(1,\{u(1)\},\emptyset)=(1,\{1\},\emptyset)$ and $T(v)=(1,\emptyset,\{v(2)\})=(1,\emptyset,\{1\})$.

As a state, $v$ is the transposition word $U=T_1\,|\,S_1=t_{12}\,|\,t_{13}$ with $R_1$ empty, $(x_1)=(1)$, active position $x=1$, $e=(1,3)$ with $C=3$, $r=s=1$, and $W_2'$ empty; its junction word is $J=T_1S_1$ with $j=1$, so the only resolution is $J_0=Z\,T_1=t_{23}\,t_{12}$ (indeed $T_xS_x=ZT_x$), and $g=0$. From $\psi=u=123$ the word $J$ exchanges $1,2$ and then $2,3$, both ascents, and $J_0$ exchanges $2,3$ and then $1,3$, both ascents; so $\mathrm{wt}_u(J_0)=\mathrm{wt}_u(J)=1$, as Lemma \ref{lemma:token}(i) asserts, and $J_0$ is a cover path by Lemma \ref{lemma:degree}. The junction move gives
$$U^+=Z\ \big|\ T_1=t_{23}\ \big|\ t_{12},\qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad 123\xrightarrow{t_{23}}132\xrightarrow{t_{12}}312,$$
a chain $u\tom2v^+\tom1w$ whose first step moves $K=2$: a chain of $\mathcal M'^{\,\mathrm{mov}}_1$, as in Proposition \ref{prop:process}(a). Its triple is $\big(1,\fx_2(123,132),\fx_1(132,312)\big)=(1,\{1\},\emptyset)=S(v)$, as required. Here $N_1(U)=\emptyset$ and $M_2(U)=\{1\}$, and the triple is $(1,u(\overline{N_1}),w(\overline{M_2}))=(1,\{1\},\emptyset)$ in the notation of the proposition.

For completeness, the other side of \eqref{eq:Bk} for this $(u,w)$: $\mathcal M_1(u,w)$ consists of the single chain $123\xrightarrow{t_{12}}213\xrightarrow{t_{13}}312$ read with the second step at level $2$ (it does not move $K$, so it is not in $\mathcal M_1^{\mathrm{mov}}$), with triple $(1,\emptyset,\fx_2(213,312))=(1,\emptyset,\{1\})=T(v)$, matching the unblocked head as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:junction}. Thus \eqref{eq:junction} reads $\{S(v)\}=\{(1,\{1\},\emptyset)\}$ on both sides, \eqref{eq:Bk} reads $\{T(v)\}\uplus\{(1,\{1\},\emptyset)\}=\{S(v)\}\uplus\{(1,\emptyset,\{1\})\}$, and Proposition \ref{prop:giambelliform} reads $(z_1-z_2)\cdot1=(y_1-z_2)-(y_1-z_1)$. The reversal of Lemma \ref{lemma:reversal} sends this configuration to the pair $w_0w=132\tom1 w_0v=231\tom1 w_0u=321$ for $(u,w)=(132,321)$, which is a blocked head, and the chain $U^+$ to the chain $132\xrightarrow{t_{12}}312\xrightarrow{t_{23}}321$ of $\mathcal M_1^{\mathrm{mov}}$ for that pair.
\end{example}

\subsubsection*{Injectivity}

\begin{lemma} \label{lemma:reversestate}
Let $U$ be a state and $U^+$ the result of the junction move, and work in $S_n$. Then the reversed transposition word $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$, read from $w_0w$ with the reversed cut, is (after reorderings as in Lemma \ref{lemma:inert}) a state for the pair $(w_0w,w_0u)$ and the weight $q$, with active position $x_g$ if $g\geq1$ and $K$ if $g=0$, and the junction move applied to it produces $U^{\mathrm{rev}}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma \ref{lemma:reversal}, $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ and $U^{\mathrm{rev}}$ are cover paths from $w_0w$ of weight $q$ with product $w^{-1}u$. Let $g\geq1$. The prefix of $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is the reversed suffix of $U^+$: the reversal of the pivot word of $W_2'$ and of $S_{e_{s+1}}\cdots S_{e_r}$, followed by $T_x\,T_{e_{s-1}}\cdots T_{e_1}\,T_{x_g}$. Its letters $T$ are those of $D$ (reversed), then $T_x,T_{e_{s-1}},\ldots,T_{e_1},T_{x_g}$; the other letters move positions disjoint from those of $D$, $x$ and $e_1,\ldots,e_{s-1}$, so by Lemma \ref{lemma:inert} they may be moved to the front, giving the form (S1) with $R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$ consisting of the reversed pivot words of the cycles of $W_2'$ other than $D$ and of $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$, the $K$-letters being $T_{y_1},\ldots,T_{y_{p}}$ with $(y_1,\ldots,y_p)=(D\text{ reversed},x,e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,x_g)$, and active position $x_g$ (which may lie in $\supp R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$). The suffix of $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is $T_{x_{g-1}}\cdots T_{x_1}\,S_{x_{j-1}}\cdots S_{x_g}\,R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$; here $S_{x_{j-1}}\cdots S_{x_g}$ followed by the reversed letters of the cycle of $R_1$ with top $C$ (if any) is the pivot factorization of a cycle $e^{\mathrm{rev}}$ with top $C$ through $x_g$, in which $x_g$ is preceded by $x_{j-1},\ldots,x_{g+1}$; moving $T_{x_{g-1}}\cdots T_{x_1}$ past it (Lemma \ref{lemma:inert}) gives the form (S2), the remaining cycles being $(x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1},K)$ and the other cycles of $R_1$, which do not contain $x_g$. The shape requirements of (S1) and (S2) are inherited from those of $U$: the cycles of $R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$ are cycles of $W_2'$ other than $D$, which by (S2) for $U$ have tops $\geq k+2$ (their tops are $\geq K$ and differ from $K$) and other elements $\leq k$, together with $(e_r,\ldots,e_{s+1},C)$; the $K$-letters $y_1,\ldots,y_{p-1}$ are positions $\leq k$ not in $\supp R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$ (the cycles of $W_2'$ are pairwise disjoint and disjoint from $e$, and $x\notin\supp W_2'$); the cycle $e^{\mathrm{rev}}$ has top $C\geq k+2$ and other elements $x_{j-1},\ldots,x_{g+1}$ and the $z$'s of the top-$C$ cycle of $R_1$, all $\leq k$; and the remaining cycles $(x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1},K)$ and those of $R_1$ consist of a top $\geq K$ and positions $\leq k$ by (S1) for $U$. Finally (S3) holds for $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$: its top-$K$ cycle $(x_1,\ldots,x_{g-1},K)$ avoids $y_1,\ldots,y_{p-1}$, because $D$ avoids $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$ by (S3) for $U$, $x=x_j$, and $e_t=x_l$ with $t<s$ forces $l>g$ (Lemma \ref{lemma:token}(ii)). So $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is a state. Its junction word is $T_{y_1}\cdots T_{y_p}\,S_{x_{j-1}}\cdots S_{x_{g+1}}\,S_{x_g}$. Suppose first that $x\leq k$. The resolution with stop at the letter $T_x$ (the letter $y_m$ with $y_m=x$) is
$$S_x\,S_{e_{s-1}}\cdots S_{e_1}\ \cdot\ (D\text{ reversed})\ \big|\ T_x\,T_{x_{j-1}}\cdots T_{x_{g+1}}\,T_{x_g}\ \cdot\ (\text{rest of the suffix}),$$
which, after moving the block $S_xS_{e_{s-1}}\cdots S_{e_1}$ past the letters of $D$ and the reversed letters of the cycle of $R_1$ with top $C$ past $T_{x_{g-1}}\cdots T_{x_1}$ (both inert), is exactly
$$U^{\mathrm{rev}}=\big(W_2'\big)^{\mathrm{rev}}\,S_{e_r}\cdots S_{e_{s+1}}\,S_xS_{e_{s-1}}\cdots S_{e_1}\ \big|\ T_xT_{x_{j-1}}\cdots T_{x_1}\,R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}.$$
Since $U^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is a cover path of weight $q$, Lemma \ref{lemma:token}(i) shows that this resolution is the one selected by the junction move. If $x=K$, there is no letter $T_x$, the sequence $(y_1,\ldots,y_p)$ is $(e_{s-1},\ldots,e_1,x_g)$, and the same comparison shows that the resolution $J_0=Z\,S_{e_{s-1}}\cdots S_{e_1}\cdot T_{x_{j-1}}\cdots T_{x_g}$ of the junction word yields $U^{\mathrm{rev}}=(W_2')^{\mathrm{rev}}S_{e_r}\cdots S_{e_{s+1}}ZS_{e_{s-1}}\cdots S_{e_1}\,|\,T_{x_{j-1}}\cdots T_{x_1}R_1^{\mathrm{rev}}$ up to inert reorderings. The case $g=0$ is identical, with the prefix of $(U^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ having no active letter after $T_{e_1}$, the suffix containing the cycle $(x_{j-1},\ldots,x_1,K,\ldots,C)$ through $K$, so that the reversed state is of $\mathcal M$ type (its $e^{\mathrm{rev}}$ has top $C\geq k+2$, contains $K$, and has all other elements $\leq k$; its $W_2'$ consists of the cycles of $R_1$ other than the one with top $C$, which by (S1) have tops $\geq k+2$ and positions $\leq k$, and (S3) is vacuous), and the resolution with stop at $T_x$ (resp.\ $J_0$ if $x=K$) again giving $U^{\mathrm{rev}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition} \label{prop:injective}
The map $\Phi$ from the sources of weight $q$ for $(u,w)$ to the targets of weight $q$ for $(u,w)$ is injective.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let $U_0$ be a source and $U_0,U_1,\ldots,U_m$ the states traversed by the process, $U_{i+1}$ being obtained from $U_i$ by a junction move followed, in case (c), by the normalization of Proposition \ref{prop:process}(c), and $U_m^+=\Phi(U_0)$ the final target. We show that $U_i$ is determined by $U_{i+1}$ (resp.\ by $U_m^+$ for $i=m$). The transposition word $U_i^+$ (the result of the move, before normalization) is determined by $U_{i+1}$: one moves the active letter $T$ of $U_{i+1}$, the last letter of its prefix, back to the front of the suffix (the normalization of (c) only reorders inert letters otherwise). By Lemma \ref{lemma:reversestate}, $(U_i^+)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is a state whose junction move produces $U_i^{\mathrm{rev}}$; since the junction move is deterministic (Lemma \ref{lemma:token}(i) selects a unique resolution), $U_i^{\mathrm{rev}}$, hence $U_i$, is determined by $U_i^+$. Finally, whether $U_i$ is the source or an intermediate state is read off from $U_i$ itself: a state is a source if and only if its active position does not lie in $\supp R_1$. Hence the whole sequence, and in particular $U_0$, is determined by $\Phi(U_0)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The junction identity] \label{theorem:junction}
The identity \eqref{eq:junction} holds for all $k\geq1$ and all $u,w\in S_\infty$. Consequently \eqref{eq:Bk}, Theorem \ref{theorem:doubledpieri} and \eqref{eq:Jk} hold for all $k\geq1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Fix $k$, $u$, $w$, a monomial $q$, and $n$ so large that everything takes place in $S_n$. Let $\mathrm{Src}(u,w)$ be the multiset of triples of the sources of weight $q$ for $(u,w)$, i.e.\ $S(v)$ for the blocked tails $v$ and the triples of the chains of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$, and $\mathrm{Tgt}(u,w)$ the multiset of $T(v)$ for the blocked heads and of the triples of the chains of $\mathcal M'^{\,\mathrm{mov}}_k$, all of weight $q$. By Propositions \ref{prop:process} and \ref{prop:injective}, $\Phi$ is an injective, triple-preserving map from sources to targets; hence $\mathrm{Src}(u,w)\subseteq\mathrm{Tgt}(u,w)$ as multisets.

Now apply the reversal of Lemma \ref{lemma:reversal} to whole configurations: it sends a compatible pair $u\tom{k}v\tom{k}w$ to the pair $w_0w\tom{k}w_0v\tom{k}w_0u$ (the reversed pivot words are pivot words of the inverse permutations, which are tight by Lemma \ref{lemma:tight}(iii)), with the same weight, with block data $(\rho_2^{-1},X^{-1},\rho_1^{-1})$ and cut $n-i$, so that $i_{\min}$ and $i_{\max}$ are exchanged with $n-i_{\max}$ and $n-i_{\min}$: blocked tails go to blocked heads and conversely. Likewise it exchanges the chains of $\mathcal M_k^{\mathrm{mov}}$ for $(u,w)$ with those of $\mathcal M'^{\,\mathrm{mov}}_k$ for $(w_0w,w_0u)$. On triples it acts by $\sigma(q,A,B)=(q,w_0B,w_0A)$, where $w_0$ acts on values: indeed $\fx_k(w_0w,w_0v)=w_0\fx_k(v,w)$, $\fx_k(w_0v,w_0u)=w_0\fx_k(u,v)$ and $(w_0v)(K)=w_0(v(K))$, so that $S(w_0v)=\sigma(T(v))$ and $T(w_0v)=\sigma(S(v))$, and similarly for the mixed chains. Hence $\sigma(\mathrm{Tgt}(u,w))=\mathrm{Src}(w_0w,w_0u)$ and $\sigma(\mathrm{Src}(u,w))=\mathrm{Tgt}(w_0w,w_0u)$. Applying the first paragraph to $(w_0w,w_0u)$ gives $\mathrm{Src}(w_0w,w_0u)\subseteq\mathrm{Tgt}(w_0w,w_0u)$, i.e.\ $\mathrm{Tgt}(u,w)\subseteq\mathrm{Src}(u,w)$. Therefore $\mathrm{Src}(u,w)=\mathrm{Tgt}(u,w)$ for every $q$, which is \eqref{eq:junction}. The consequences follow from Propositions \ref{prop:junction} and \ref{prop:doubledreduction}.
\end{proof}



\section{Proof of the parabolic reduction} \label{section:pw}

In this section we prove Proposition \ref{prop:parabolic}. Peterson's comparison formula expresses the Gromov--Witten invariants of $G/P$ in terms of those of $G/B$; it was proved by Woodward \cite{woodward2005d} and extended to equivariant quantum cohomology by Huang and Li \cite{huang2015equivariant}, whose form we use. We use the notation of Section \ref{section:definitions}: $W_P$ is a parabolic subgroup of $S_n$ with $P=\pcomp P$, $\omega_P=w_P$ is its longest element, and for $\lambda_P\in Q^\vee/Q^\vee_P$ we write $\lambda_B\in Q^\vee$ for its Peterson--Woodward lift. In the language of roots, $\lambda_B$ is the unique lift with $\langle\alpha,\lambda_B\rangle\in\{0,-1\}$ for all positive roots $\alpha$ of the Levi subgroup of $P$; in type $A$ these are the roots $\alpha_i+\cdots+\alpha_{j-1}$ with $s_i,\ldots,s_{j-1}\in P$, and $\langle\alpha_i+\cdots+\alpha_{j-1},d\rangle=\langle d\rangle_{ij}$, so this is the condition of Definition \ref{def:pwlift}.

\begin{proposition}[{\cite[Proposition~2.10]{huang2015equivariant}}] \label{prop:huangli}
Let $W_P$ be a parabolic subgroup of $W$ and $u,v,w\in W^P$. Then for any $\lambda_P\in Q^\vee/Q_P^\vee$, we have that
$$c_{u,v}^{w,\lambda_P}(y;y) = c_{u,v}^{w\omega_P\omega_{P'},\lambda_B}(y;y),$$
where $\omega_P$ is the longest element in $W_P$, $\omega_{P'}$ is the longest element in $W_{P'}$, where $P'$ is the parabolic subgroup generated by $\{s_\alpha\mid \alpha\in\Delta_P,\langle \alpha, \lambda_B\rangle=0\}$, and $\lambda_B$ is the Peterson-Woodward lift of $\lambda_P$.
\end{proposition}

Here the left-hand side is a structure constant of $QH_T^*(G/P)$, i.e.\ of the parabolic quantum double Schubert polynomials $\qsch^P_w(x;y)$ of Definition \ref{def:parabolic}, which represent the Schubert classes of $QH_T^*(G/P)$ \cite{lam2014quantum}, and the right-hand side is a structure constant of $QH_T^*(G/B)$.

\begin{corollary} \label{corollary:pwz}
With the notation of Proposition \ref{prop:huangli}, for all $u,v,w\in W^P$,
$$c_{u,v}^{w,\lambda_P}(y;z) = c_{u,v}^{w\omega_P\omega_{P'},\lambda_B}(y;z).$$
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The parabolic polynomials satisfy the same quantum Cauchy identity as in Lemma \ref{lemma:quantumtoordinary}. Indeed, by \cite[Lemma~18]{lam2014quantum}, $\qsch^P_w(x;y)=\theta^P(\sch_w(x;y))$ for $w\in W^P$, where $\theta^P$ is the parabolic quantization map of Ciocan-Fontanine \cite{ciocan1999partial}, extended linearly over the coefficient variables; applying $\theta^P$ to the classical identity $\sch_v(x;z)=\sum_{v_1\cdot v_2=v}\sch_{v_1}(y;z)\sch_{v_2}(x;y)$ gives $\qsch^P_v(x;z)=\sum_{v_1\cdot v_2=v}\sch_{v_1}(y;z)\qsch^P_{v_2}(x;y)$, where we note that $v_2\in W^P$ for every reduced factorization (see below), so that $\theta^P(\sch_{v_2}(x;y))$ is indeed $\qsch^P_{v_2}(x;y)$. Hence
$$c_{u,v}^{w,\lambda_P}(y;z) = \sum_{v_1\cdot v_2=v}c_{u,v_2}^{w,\lambda_P}(y;y)\,\sch_{v_1}(y;z).$$
Here, if $v=v_1\cdot v_2$ is reduced and $v\in W^P$, then $v_2\in W^P$: for $s_i\in P$, $v_2s_i<v_2$ would give $\ell(vs_i)\leq\ell(v_1)+\ell(v_2s_i)<\ell(v)$, contradicting $v\in W^P$. Thus Proposition \ref{prop:huangli} applies to each term, and resumming by Lemma \ref{lemma:quantumtoordinary} gives
$$\sum_{v_1\cdot v_2=v}c_{u,v_2}^{w\omega_P\omega_{P'},\lambda_B}(y;y)\,\sch_{v_1}(y;z) = c_{u,v}^{w\omega_P\omega_{P'},\lambda_B}(y;z).\qedhere$$
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition \ref{prop:parabolic}]
Let $d_P\in Q^\vee/Q^\vee_P$ with Peterson--Woodward lift $d=\lambda_B$. In type $A$, $\langle\alpha_i,d\rangle=2d_i-d_{i-1}-d_{i+1}=\langle d\rangle_i$, so the parabolic subgroup $P'$ of Proposition \ref{prop:huangli} is generated by $\{s_i\in P\mid\langle d\rangle_i=0\}$, that is, by $\{s_i\mid i\in z_P(d)\}$, and $\omega_P\omega_{P'}=w_Pw_{z_P(d)}$. The claim is therefore Corollary \ref{corollary:pwz}. (The passage from $S_n$ to $S_\infty$, i.e.\ from $P$ to $\pcomp P$, is the stability discussed after Definition \ref{def:parabolic}.)
\end{proof}

\appendix
\section{Proof of the positivity theorem} \label{appendix:positivity}
\begin{center}
\textsc{by Tongren Xiao and Yihua Yue}
\end{center}

This appendix proves Theorem \ref{theorem:positivity}. The argument is taken from \cite{xiaoyuequantumpositivity}; it combines the geometric argument of Mihalcea \cite{mihalcea2006positivity} with the Graham-positivity theorem of Gao and Xiong \cite{gao2025grahampositivitytripleschubert} for $B^-$-stable effective cycles.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem \ref{theorem:positivity}]
Our product is written with the output basis carrying the parameters of the second factor, so commutativity of multiplication identifies the coefficient $C_{v,u}^{w,d}(z,y)$ below with $c_{u,v}^{w,d}(y;z)$. It is therefore enough to prove that $C_{u,v}^{w,d}(y,t)$ is in $\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}[t_i-y_j]$.

Fix $u,v,w\in S_n$, increasing $n$ if necessary so that all permutations and parameters occurring in the coefficient under consideration are included. Write
$\mathbf a=(t_1,\ldots,t_n,y_1,\ldots,y_n)$, and let $\tau\in S_{2n}$ interchange the two blocks, so that $\tau\mathbf a=(y_1,\ldots,y_n,t_1,\ldots,t_n)$. Regard the product expansion in $S_{2n}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:xiaoyue-product}
\qsch_u(x;\tau\mathbf a)\qsch_v(x;\mathbf a)
=\sum_{w\in S_{2n}}C_{u,v}^{w,\tau}(\mathbf a;q)\qsch_w(x;\mathbf a).
\end{equation}
We recall the geometric interpretation of these coefficients. Let $e_i(x)$ and $E_i(x,q)$ denote, respectively, the elementary symmetric polynomials and their quantum analogues in $2n$ variables, and set
\[
J_{\mathbf a}^{2n}=\langle e_i(x)-e_i(\mathbf a):1\leq i\leq 2n\rangle,\qquad
J_{q\mathbf a}^{2n}=\langle E_i(x,q)-e_i(\mathbf a):1\leq i\leq 2n\rangle.
\]
The Fomin--Gelfand--Postnikov deformation map \cite{fomin1997quantum}, extended $\mathbb Z[\mathbf a]$-linearly, induces
\[
\overline{\mathcal Q}:\mathbb Z[x,\mathbf a]/J_{\mathbf a}^{2n}
\longrightarrow \mathbb Z[x,\mathbf a,q]/J_{q\mathbf a}^{2n}.
\]
The classical Borel presentation identifies the source with $H_T^*(X)$, and Kim's presentation \cite{kim1996equivariant} identifies the target with $QH_T^*(X)$, where $X=\mathrm{Fl}_{2n}$. Under the Lam--Shimozono identification \cite{lam2014quantum} of quantum double Schubert polynomials with quantum Schubert classes, \eqref{eq:xiaoyue-product} is the quantum equivariant Littlewood--Richardson expansion for the classes obtained by applying $\overline{\mathcal Q}$ to the first factor. Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:xiaoyue-eqlr}
C_{u,v}^{w,\tau}(\mathbf a;q)
=\sum_{d\geq0}q^d(\pi_{M_d})^T_*
\left(
\operatorname{ev}_1^*[\tau X_u]^T\,
\operatorname{ev}_2^*[X_v]^T\,
\operatorname{ev}_3^*[Y_w]^T
\right).
\end{equation}
Here $M_d=\overline M_{0,3}(X,d)$, $\operatorname{ev}_i:M_d\to X$ are the evaluation maps, $\pi_{M_d}:M_d\to\mathrm{pt}$ is the structure map, and $X_r$ and $Y_r$ denote the Schubert and opposite Schubert varieties indexed by $r$.

For each effective degree $d$, put
\[
F=(\operatorname{ev}_1,\operatorname{ev}_2):M_d\longrightarrow X\times X,\qquad
I_{u,v}^d=F^{-1}(\tau X_u\times X_v),\qquad
\Gamma_{u,v}^d=(\operatorname{ev}_3)_*[I_{u,v}^d].
\]
The incidence scheme $I_{u,v}^d$ is reduced and locally irreducible, and all its irreducible components have codimension $\ell(u)+\ell(v)$. Indeed, the moduli space $M_d$ is irreducible by Thomsen's theorem \cite{thomsen1998irreducibility}, and Mihalcea's incidence lemma \cite[Lemma~6.3]{mihalcea2006positivity} applies after diagonal translation by $a_0=1\times w_0\in S_{2n}$, where $w_0$ is the longest element in the second block: it identifies $\tau X_u\times X_v$ with $w_0^{(2n)}X_u\times X_v$. The codimension is therefore
\[
\operatorname{codim}_X(w_0^{(2n)}X_u)+\ell(v)=\ell(u)+\ell(v).
\]
By the incidence formula of Mihalcea \cite[Section~3.1]{mihalcea2006positivity}, this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:xiaoyue-incidence}
\operatorname{ev}_1^*[\tau X_u]^T\,
\operatorname{ev}_2^*[X_v]^T\cap[M_d]^T=[I_{u,v}^d]_T.
\end{equation}

The cycle $\Gamma_{u,v}^d$ is effective and $B^-(\tau)$-invariant. To see invariance, write $N^-(\tau)=N^-\cap\tau N^-\tau^{-1}$ and $B^-(\tau)=TN^-(\tau)$. The varieties $X_v$ and $\tau X_u$ are stable under the corresponding actions, so their product, its inverse image under the equivariant map $F$, and its proper push-forward under $\operatorname{ev}_3$ are $B^-(\tau)$-invariant. Effectivity follows from the reducedness of $I_{u,v}^d$.

There is a unique expansion
\[
[\Gamma_{u,v}^d]_T=\sum_{r\in S_{2n}}p_{u,v}^{r,d}(\mathbf a)[X_r]_T
\quad\text{in }H_T^*(X).
\]
By Gao and Xiong's Graham--positivity theorem for $B^-(\tau)$-invariant effective cycles \cite[Theorem~2.4 and Corollary~2.5]{gao2025grahampositivitytripleschubert},
\[
p_{u,v}^{r,d}(\mathbf a)\in
\mathbb Z_{\geq0}[t_i-y_j:1\leq i,j\leq n].
\]
Finally, by equivariant Poincaré duality, \eqref{eq:xiaoyue-incidence}, and the projection formula,
\[
(\operatorname{ev}_3)^T_*
\left(\operatorname{ev}_1^*[\tau X_u]^T
\operatorname{ev}_2^*[X_v]^T\right)
=[\Gamma_{u,v}^d]_T,
\]
and hence the degree-$d$ summand of \eqref{eq:xiaoyue-eqlr} is
\[
(\pi_X)^T_*\bigl([\Gamma_{u,v}^d]_T[Y_w]^T\bigr)
=\sum_{r\in S_{2n}}p_{u,v}^{r,d}(\mathbf a)
(\pi_X)^T_*\bigl([X_r]^T[Y_w]^T\bigr)
=p_{u,v}^{w,d}(\mathbf a).
\]
Thus every coefficient $C_{u,v}^{w,d}(y,t)$ is in
$\mathbb Z_{\geq0}[t_i-y_j]$. Replacing $(u,v,y,t)$ by $(v,u,z,y)$ proves the asserted positivity of $c_{u,v}^{w,d}(y;z)$.
\end{proof}
